Symphonica 64
Welcome to Symphonica 64! This is a zany text adventure based on the 8-bit retro community and its history.

Homepage: http://www.symphonica64.com

The puzzles are logical, if a little surreal, and there are lots of clues, so you can play it even if you don't "get" many of the references.

This is the competition build.

That means that the game cannot be finished without two codes that you will get by watching http://fb.me/BITSymphonic, @C64Audio or http://www.backintimesymphonic.com

The first person to finish this adventure and send a saved game to a designated email address will win a prize. We will check both the send and receipt timestamps of the email in making this decision.

Type "help" for more hints. Type "world" for the nice world map, and type "map" for the Symphonica map, upon which this game is loosely beased.

Credits: written by Chris Abbott, Anna Black and Damian Manning. Art by Trevor Storey and Toni Galvez. Voice by Booming Boz! Additional material from K8Bit, Chris Van Graas, Simon Jones, Andy Roberts and many fans :) Thanks to MJ Hibbett and the C64 Mafia!

This game is a comedy parody. Any resemblance of any characters or places in this game to real-life is purely coincidental.

Symphonic Penn Avenue
You are on a leafy avenue. The entrance to The Symphonica Amphitheatre is south, and Penn Avenue stretches zanily east and west.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are east and west.

> code 348783654876
Somewhere to the west, a Kickstarter delivered!

> zone wastelands
You find yourself in a hive of SID and villainy.

Sidventure Port
You are at the south end of Sidventure port.

In a decrepit but surprisingly vibrant marketplace, various remixers, website owners and demo pirates are milling around swapping their wares and complaining about used equipment prices.

A group of background stall owners attracts your attention, including Marvin Droogsma selling C65s at a knock down price. You still can't afford one though.

There are three Kickstarters for C64 books in the corner, all shouting at each other and trying to interview you. Sample copies are strewn around.  Also here is an Ocean storybook and a Bunstock.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Uncle Clive and Mat the Merchant are here.  Retrograde is here on his stall, trying to sell you two copies of Chase HQ.  Exits are north, east, south and west.

> take storybook
Ok, you pick up the Ocean storybook.

> zone symphonica
You have moved back to a warm cosy pub.

05. Ben And Ratt (Back Room)
This small back room is filled with a surprising amount of 80s computers, packets of fags, and some beer. William Wobbler is wobbling along on one screen, and James Bond cavorts across another. There is a shocking lack of humour here.  Also here is a Hubbard flute.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  An exit leads east.

> East
You move east.

05.The Ben And Ratt
This large and chunky pub has some smooth scrolls on the wall and now has a family atmosphere. Various performers stand in the corners performing to their own little crowds. One performer is juggling joysticks, and another is singing an opera about 6502 based cross-compilation.  Also here is a nice joint and a tasty pub piano.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Ben the Jester and The FastLoaders are here.  Exits are south and west.

> South
You move south.

1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are north, south and west.

Unnamed River Bank
You're still on the bank of the "Damn, I wish we'd remembered to put a label on this" River running between Sidventure and the Wastelands. It's crunchy underfoot, but you don't let the fragile dead bone pile bother you.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are north, south and west.

The nomenclaturically challenged River Bank
You're halfway down the bank of the "I knew I should have told Trevor to put a label on" River running between Sidventure and the Wastelands.

The ground makes hamster-squeaking sounds as you walk. It might be your shoes, though, not the almost-dead pile of beige warm hamsters underfoot.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are north and south.

> South
You move south.

The namingly neglected River Bank
Still on a river bank.

The ground makes an unusual chirping noise as you walk. Sure, birds originated from Dinosaurs, but you don't expect the fossils to go around chirping. Stupid fossils.  Also here is a strangely lively hamster and a stupid fossil.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are north and south.

> South
You move south.

River Bank near Wastelands exit

12. The Follin And Fettle
This pub, befitting its name, is bustling with individuality and is in fine fettle. It's filled with fine frames. The forgetful figure of Follin furtively fidgets with his fetlock.

> give deal to follinator

04. The TDK Bar
A fetching shade of purple adorns the walls. A meaty stage dwarfs the bar. There is a mingling background crowd, but no one you'd want to actually examine. Moths flit about. I'm surprised someone hasn't killed them.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Sir TDK and Cheeky Hibbett are here.  An exit leads east.

> give rampack to hibbett
He looks thrilled, and jumps on stage to sing his signature number (and now a stage show) Hey Hey 16k. You collect the score from him.

> zone wastelands
You find yourself in a hive of SID and villainy.

Mountain Pass #2
High above the land of Mystery and Fantasy on a mountain pass, there's... you. Gold Singing Lessons
On a mountain plain, in a ramshackle.. er... shack, there's a sign: "Find yourself tongue-tied when attempting to vocalise the praises of precious metals in the form of song? Educate yourself to your potential here, but learning both the form and structure of cantations about the subject of Gold".

The sign is bigger than the shack.  Also here is a gold-singing lesson.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are north and south.

> South
You move south.

Mountain Pass #1
You're on a high mountain pass, way above the Reyn Plains. When you look down the people look like ants, and the ants look like smaller ants. This is probably due to physics.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Trev the Storey is here.  Exits are north and south.

> South
You move south.

Thing
1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Thing with no Spring is here. He looks pretty miserable, and has lost his bounce.  Exits are north, east and south.

> East
You move east.

Tim The Troll
You are in a ruined palace. A decrepit sign says "Edge edge edge edge", so the character that lives here is obviously living on the edge. There is a dartboard here with some electronic arts on, and a picture of Garfield with kisses on it. I'd advise you don't ask about that.

1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Tim the Troll is here.  An exit leads west.

> give edge to tim
Tim looks at the infringing Edge, and begins to fume. You could swear there was steam coming out of his ears. He produces a phone from his pocket and calls some lawyers. Then more laywers. Then more. Getting angrier and angrier, he explodes in a puff of trademarks.

You pick up the sadly singed Wizardry score.

> zone symphonica
You have moved back to a warm cosy pub.

The Boring Bridge
A featureless, unimaginative bridge. It's seriously bland. I'd just use it for crossing if I were you. It goes from one side to the other side. Nothing interesting here. If it was a game, it would have a Zzap!64 score of 63%: not disastrous but only worth five minutes. It's the game equivalent of cafeteria mashed potato, or Commodore User.

In fact, this bridge is annoyingly boring. The sheer bridgeness is pointed in its lack of interesting features, to the extent that it's _actively_ boring, not just passively boring. Frankly, I get angry just thinking about it!! What a boring bridge! Stop being boring!!!  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are east and west.

> East
You move east.

All hail Stesha.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  An exposed Karateka, Stesha the Manga Stesher and a retro group of Karatekas are here.  An exit leads west.

In a decrepit but surprisingly vibrant marketplace, various remixers, website owners and demo pirates are milling around swapping their wares and complaining about used equipment prices.

Also here is a Bunstock.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Uncle Clive and Mat the Merchant are here.  Retrograde is here on his stall, trying to sell you two copies of Chase HQ. Sidventure Port
You are at the south end of Sidventure port.

In a decrepit but surprisingly vibrant marketplace, various remixers, website owners and demo pirates are milling around swapping their wares and complaining about used equipment prices.

A group of background stall owners attracts your attention, including Marvin Droogsma selling C65s at a knock down price. You still can't afford one though.

There are three Kickstarters for C64 books in the corner, all shouting at each other and trying to interview you. Sample copies are strewn around.  Also here is a Bunstock.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Uncle Clive and Mat the Merchant are here.  Retrograde is here on his stall, trying to sell you two copies of Chase HQ.  Exits are north, east, south and west.

> East
You move east.

The Compunet Room
A forgotten corner of the map, this deserted room contains pictures of people tapping away on an online system that died a long time ago.

You can see pictures of celebrities of long past: Ash 'n Dave throwing snowballs at each other, Chain and Froodle doing the Froodle dance on a C2N, and Ian & Mic stroking a lizard.

Go on, check your mailbox. For old time's sake.

1980s Barry Leitch feels at home here, and lounges in a chair while he waits.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Exits are north and west.

> North
You move north.

A Danish bartender designs some new booze designs in the corner while complaining that he's out of Vodka, **** is ****ing **** in a ****, an extremely censored Psycho is tapping away on a keyboard with missing keys doing a demo that fits into one byte, and a big sign on the wall says "There's no place like ****".  Also here is a Swedish-Canadian CRT.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  LAndroid and The Censored Dragon are here.  An exit leads south.

The Boring Bridge
A featureless, unimaginative bridge. It's seriously bland. I'd just use it for crossing if I were you. It goes from one side to the other side. Nothing interesting here. If it was a game, it would have a Zzap!64 score of 63%: not disastrous but only worth five minutes. It's the game equivalent of cafeteria mashed potato, or Commodore User.

In fact, this bridge is annoyingly boring. The sheer bridgeness is pointed in its lack of interesting features, to the extent that it's _actively_ boring, not just passively boring. 01. The Hubbard Cupboard
A smart pub with a long history, this serves only the best Northern ales. There are pictures of dragons and spaceships on the walls, and a sign saying "There's no place like Hull".  Also here is an organ stand and a comfy sofa.  The Mighty Rob is here.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Rory is here, glaring at you angrily.  An exit leads south.

The Mighty Rob writes a loading tune that makes you cry.

> talk to rob
Rob is pleased to talk about how Delta came into being, and while illustrating the arpeggios with both hands, drops a Delta score.

The Mighty Rob makes you happy with a pitch bend.

> take delta score
Ok, you pick up the Delta score.

The Mighty Rob writes an Amstrad tune, but it's only for the money.

> get moustache from rob
"Ow!!". The Mighty Rob starts to change, and vanishes in a puff of SID. In front of you is now... the Amazing Rob!

He's doing a Sega Genesis soundtrack and writing some jazz.

> talk to rob
Rob is pleased you enquired. He gives you a quick update on his Sega Genesis work, dropping a rousing soundtrack as he does (this counts as a score).

> take soundtrack
Ok, you pick up the rousing Sega Genesis soundtrack.

> zone wastelands
You find yourself in a hive of SID and villainy.

Sidventure Port
You are at the south end of Sidventure port.

In a decrepit but surprisingly vibrant marketplace, various remixers, website owners and demo pirates are milling around swapping their wares and complaining about used equipment prices.

A group of background stall owners attracts your attention, including Marvin Droogsma selling C65s at a knock down price. You still can't afford one though.

There are three Kickstarters for C64 books in the corner, all shouting at each other and trying to interview you. Sample copies are strewn around.  Also here is a Bunstock.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Rory is here, glaring at you angrily.  Uncle Clive and Mat the Merchant are here.  Retrograde is here on his stall, trying to sell you two copies of Chase HQ.  Exits are north, east, south and west.

> South
You move south.

Anonymous River Bank
You're on a northernmost bank of the "We forgot to name this" River running between Sidventure and the Wastelands. It's a bit muddy. Squelching is happening.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Rory is here, being generically vicious.  Exits are north and south.

> South
You move south.

Unnamed River Bank
You're still on the bank of the "Damn, I wish we'd remembered to put a label on this" River running between Sidventure and the Wastelands. You move south.

Yee Oldee SID n Vic

What a hive of SID and villainy this is! The chap from C64 Tapper serves drinks to the Dragon from Forbidden Forest.

Oh oh, the C64 and Spectrum owners slowly stop talking, and look open-mouthed at the Amstrad user.

"Burn the Witch!"
"He's not got dedicated video hardware!"
"His keys are too hard!".
"Zzap him!"
"Crash his ass!"
"Format his Commodores!" says a lone voice, which trails off embarrassingly.

The Amstrad user darts out of the door, followed by the Spectrum and C64 users.

"Fight! Fight!"

Sidventure Port
You are at the south end of Sidventure port.

In a decrepit but surprisingly vibrant marketplace, various remixers, website owners and demo pirates are milling around swapping their wares and complaining about used equipment prices.

A group of background stall owners attracts your attention, including Marvin Droogsma selling C65s at a knock down price. You still can't afford one though.

There are three Kickstarters for C64 books in the corner, all shouting at each other and trying to interview you. Sample copies are strewn around.  Also here is a Bunstock.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Uncle Clive, Mat the Merchant and Garou the fluffy doggie are here.  Konkey Dong is Konging in a corner.  Retrograde is here on his stall, trying to sell you two copies of Chase HQ.  Exits are north, east, south and west.

> South
You move south.

You're on a northernmost bank of the "We forgot to name this" River running between Sidventure and the Wastelands. It's a bit muddy. Squelching is happening.  1980s Barry Leitch is following you.  Konkey Dong is Konging in a corner.  Garou the fluffy doggie is here.  Exits are north and south.

Don't forget: this adventure game is part of the Back in Time Symphonic Collection campaign to get C64 music played in concert halls across the world. We want it played by the London Symphony at the Royal Albert Hall.

Please pre-order/back our efforts at http://www.backintimesymphonic.com (cheers!)
